<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the first lattice correction to Bloch's law for the three-dimensional nearest-neighbor quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet at every fixed spin $`S=\tfrac12,1,\tfrac32,\ldots`$. The spontaneous-magnetization deficit agrees with the full ideal-magnon density up to $`o(\beta^{-5/2})`$. In addition to the leading Bloch term, this gives the correction $`3\zeta(5/2)(\beta S)^{-5/2}/(128\pi^{3/2})`$. The magnetization is the right derivative at zero field of the thermodynamic pressure; the volume limit precedes the field derivative, and the low-temperature limit is taken last.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed $S\in\{\tfrac12,1,\tfrac32,\ldots\}$, $$\lim_{\beta\to\infty}(\beta S)^{5/2} \bigl[S-m_S(\beta)-n_S^{\mathrm{sw}}(\beta)\bigr]=0.$$
  - Explanation: At fixed spin, the spontaneous magnetization deficit agrees with the full lattice ideal-magnon density to the stated low-temperature precision.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-expansion)): For every fixed positive half-integer $S$, $$S-m_S(\beta) =\frac{\zeta(3/2)}{8\pi^{3/2}}(\beta S)^{-3/2} +\frac{3\zeta(5/2)}{128\pi^{3/2}}(\beta S)^{-5/2} +o\bigl((\beta S)^{-5/2}\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: The magnetization deficit has a leading Bloch term and an explicit first lattice correction.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-loopinputs)): The following statements hold at every finite volume and $\beta>0$.* 1. *If $q_\Lambda$ is the number of slot edges and $c(\omega)$ the number of cycles, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-\beta H_{S,\Lambda}} =e^{\beta q_\Lambda/4}\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}2^{c(\omega)}.$$ The spectral distribution of the total $z$-spin is obtained by the unpinned color law, assigning $+1/2$ to each up slot and $-1/2$ to each down slot at time zero. The expectation, in this normalized color law, of the exponential of $\beta$ times the field times this sum is the ratio of the field and zero-field partition functions.* 2. *For the allowed sets $E,P$ and point $i$ above, $$\label{eq:pinreturn} \mathbb P_E(i\in\mathcal B_E)=\frac{2r}{1+r}, \qquad d_E(i)=\frac{r}{1+r},\qquad r=\mathbb E_Pq_i.$$* 3. *More generally, let $T$ be a nonempty finite set of points at one deterministic time, disjoint from an allowed $D$, and put $P'=D\cup T$. Expose $\mathcal B_{P'}$ and let $q_j^{D,T}$ be the fresh-walk probability from $j\in T$ of hitting $T$ at positive elapsed time before $D$. Then $$\label{eq:simultaneouspin} \mathbb P_D(T\subset\mathcal B_D)\le (2r_T)^{\|T\|}, \qquad r_T=\frac1{\|T\|}\sum_{j\in T}\mathbb E_{P'}q_j^{D,T}.$$* 4. *For any allowed $D$, the factorial measure of $j$ distinct inter-site marks under $\mathbb P_D$ is bounded by $2^j$ times the base factorial measure. This holds with chronological ordering and with arbitrary deterministic restrictions on the edges and times.
  - Explanation: The cycle representation supplies the partition function, color observables, pin identities, and mark-count bounds.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-kernelenergy)): At fixed exposure, every killed interval kernel and its adjoint are substochastic in both counting sums and contract counting $\ell^p$ for $p=1,2,\infty$. Between instantaneous operations a propagated vector satisfies $$\frac{d}{dt}\lVert v_t\rVert_2^2=-2\mathcal D_t(v_t).$$ The squared norm cannot increase at an instantaneous operation.* *Let $U$ be a one-period kernel with any allowed pin killings, and let $y$ vanish on its cut killing slots. Set $e_y=\langle y,(I-U)y\rangle\ge0$. For either $U_d=U$ or $U^*$, let $Y_t$ be the adjoint propagation of $y$ from the terminal cut to an intermediate slice. Then $$\label{eq:energy} \int_{\mathrm{traversal}}\mathcal D_t(Y_t)\,dt\le e_y, \qquad \lVert y-U_d^*y\rVert_2^2\le2e_y.$$ The contraction and local norm-loss statements hold with extra spatial killing and for intervals continued across a period cut. Equation <a href="#eq:energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy">[eq:energy]</a> concerns one complete traversal; each copy of that traversal has the same bound.
  - Explanation: Killed propagation and its adjoint contract counting norms, and their norm loss controls Dirichlet energy.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-comparison)): For every fixed positive half-integer $S$ there are $\beta_0(S)<\infty$ and a function $\varepsilon_S(\beta)\to0$ as $\beta\to\infty$ such that the following holds for every $\beta\ge\beta_0(S)$. Let $\Lambda$ be any finite cube, let $E=D_0\cup F$ with $F$ any finite deterministic set of slot-time pins, and let $i=(x,a)$ be a time-zero post-seam slot. Suppose that $B_{50R_*}(x)$ is interior to $\Lambda$ and that $E$ has no point above this ball. Then $$\label{eq:pinnedcomparison} \lvert d_E(i)-\frac 1\ell n_S^{\rm sw}(\beta)\rvert \le \varepsilon_S(\beta)\beta^{-5/2}.$$ The same uniform error bound, after enlarging $\varepsilon_S$ if necessary, holds for $\lvert \mathbb E_{E\cup\{i\}}q_i-\ell^{-1}n_S^{\rm sw}(\beta)\rvert$.
  - Explanation: Far from allowed pins and the boundary, the down probability and averaged return probability approach the ideal density uniformly.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-capacity)): For the preceding data, put $y=f-h$. Then $$\begin{aligned} A^*f&=(1-q)\eta, \label{eq:harmonic}\\ e_y&=\langle y,\mu_-\rangle, \label{eq:errorenergy}\\ q-(1-c) &=\langle h,\mu_+\rangle+\langle y,\mu_+\rangle. \label{eq:capacityidentity} \end{aligned}$$ No assumption of almost-sure killing is required.* *Suppose, in addition, that $\lVert h\rVert_\infty\le M$ and there are numbers $X,\varepsilon\ge0$ such that, for both $d=+,-$, $$\label{eq:abstractdual} \lvert \langle \mu_d,z\rangle\rvert\le X\sqrt{e_z}+\varepsilon \quad\text{whenever }z\|_K=0\text{ and }\lVert z\rVert_\infty\le1+M.$$ Then $$\label{eq:capacitystability} \lvert q-(1-c)\rvert \le \lvert \langle h,\mu_+\rangle\rvert+2X^2+2\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: Forward and adjoint residuals compare a trial potential with the true hitting potential without requiring almost-sure killing.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-geometryinputs)): For all sufficiently large $\beta$, every allowed pin set $D$, every deterministic time, every anchor $x$, and every $R_0$ above a fixed threshold, the following bounds hold. Put $p=(5/\beta)^{10/11}$.* 1. *The probability of a nonregular time under $\mathbb P_D$ is at most $$\label{eq:regularityprob} C R_0^3p^6+C e^{-cR_0^\xi},$$ for some fixed $\xi>0$. Failure of the second regularity condition alone has probability at most $C e^{-cR_0^\xi}$.* 2. *At every locally regular time, every nonnegative vector on the hole graph supported above $B_{R_0}(x)$ satisfies $$\label{eq:nash} \lVert v\rVert_2^{2+4/3}\le C\mathcal D_t(v)\lVert v\rVert_1^{4/3}.$$ There is an available slot within bounded distance of every site in $B_{2R_0}(x)$. Available slots over two sites in this ball at distance $b$ can be joined by a path of length at most $C(1+b)$, staying within distance $C(1+b)$ of the first site and inside $B_{3R_0}(x)$.* 3. *At every regular time, each available slot $a$ above $B_{R_0}(x)$ admits a unit flow $J_a$ to infinity on the extended hole graph. For $b$ the distance between its source site and an edge, $$\label{eq:flowenvelopes} \|J_a(e)\|\le C(1+b)^{-199/100}, \qquad \|J_a(e)\|\le C(1+b)^{-2}\quad(b\le R_0).$$* *All constants are uniform in the volume, pins, anchor, and deterministic time.
  - Explanation: Imported geometric estimates provide regularity probabilities, local diffusion, short paths, and flows to infinity.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-snapshot)): For every allowed pin set $D$ and every set $T$ of distinct slot points at one deterministic time, with $1\le \|T\|\le6$, $$\label{eq:snapshot} \mathbb P_D(T\subset\mathcal B_D)\le C\lambda^{\|T\|}.$$ The bound is uniform in the finite cube, the pin set, the time, and the points, including distinct slots at the same site.
  - Explanation: A deterministic snapshot has joint obstacle probabilities bounded by the corresponding powers of the diffusive density.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-isolatedpath)): Fix an allowed pin set $P$, a deterministic slot $v$, and an interval $[s,t]$ of positive length less than $\beta$ containing no seam in its interior. Follow the picture’s strand $X$ from $v$ at time $s$ to time $t$. Let $\mathcal I$ be the event that this strand is exposed and, throughout the interval, no other exposed slot lies at its site or a neighboring site, on both sides of every jump. For every measurable path event $\mathcal A$ consisting of paths that stay in the interior, $$\label{eq:isolateddomination} \mathbb P_P\bigl(\mathcal I\cap\{X\in\mathcal A\}\bigr) \le2\mathbb E_{\mathrm b}\mathbf 1_{\{X\in\mathcal A\}}.$$ On the right, $X$ is the base tagged walk, with rate $1/2$ along each slot edge; on interior paths its law is the free walk law. Any seam at an endpoint is excluded: use the post-seam side at $s$ and the pre-seam side at $t$. The same bound holds when both paths are traced backward from a prescribed slot at $t$ to $s$, with these same chronological sides.
  - Explanation: The joint event of isolation and a prescribed strand path is dominated by the base tagged-walk law.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-good)): Under $\mathbb P_P$ there is an event $\mathcal G$ with $$\label{eq:goodprob} \mathbb P_P(\mathcal G^c)=o(\beta^{-5/2})$$ having the following properties in $B_{12R_*}(x)$, in either direction of traversal.* 1. *A strand makes at most $c_0L$ inter-site steps in a block and at most $C\log\beta$ in a bin. The bounds also apply to segments traced from an intermediate visit to the region, in either time direction.* 2. *At each block cut, every ball of radius $c_1L$ centered in the region contains at most five obstacle slots. Local regularity at radius $R_*$ about $x$ holds at all times. In every block, the set of fully regular times at that radius has length at least half the block.* 3. *A strand is called isolated in a block and direction if no other obstacle slot lies within $c_2L$ of its starting site. Every such strand stays at distance greater than two from all other obstacles and has maximal site displacement at most $CL^{3/4}$ in the block.* *The constants are fixed in the order $c_0$, then $c_2$, then $c_1$, with each sufficiently large for the preceding choices. The event and all its properties repeat on subsequent copies of the period.
  - Explanation: A good exposure supplies sparse obstacles, regular slices, step bounds, and controlled displacement of isolated strands; its complement has the stated little-oh bound.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-heatbound)): On $\mathcal G$, a fresh-walk interval kernel of elapsed length $u$, additionally killed on exit from $B_{R_*}(x)$, has maximum entry at most $$\label{eq:heatbound} C(1+u)^{-3/2}.$$ The bound holds for either time direction, for every starting slice, with additional spatial killing, and across copies of the period.
  - Explanation: On a good exposure, localized propagation has a diffusive upper bound in either time direction.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-localization)): On $\mathcal G$, from any slot above $B_{6R}(x)$ and any starting slice, a fresh walk run for elapsed time $u\le2\beta$ in either direction stays within site distance $$\label{eq:localization} C(1+u/L)L^{3/4}$$ of its starting site, apart from a rapidly small probability uniform over the fixed good exposure. The assertion bounds the probability of excessive displacement before killing. In particular, adding killing on exit from $B_{R_*}(x)$ changes these propagations by a rapidly small amount in total mass.
  - Explanation: On a fixed good exposure, the fresh walk has a uniform displacement envelope apart from a rapidly small failure probability.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-flowoverlap)): At a fully regular slice on $\mathcal G$, choose the unit flows in <a href="#eq:flowenvelopes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flowenvelopes">[eq:flowenvelopes]</a> for available slots $a,a'$ over $B_{6R}(x)$. Then $$\label{eq:flowoverlap} \sum_e\|J_a(e)J_{a'}(e)\| \le\frac{C}{1+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(a,a')},$$ where the distance is between their sites.
  - Explanation: Unit flows from separated source sites have overlap bounded inversely by their site separation.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-freebounds)): For fixed $S$ and all sufficiently large $\beta$, the following bounds hold uniformly in the direction, elapsed time, site, and slot. For the full smooth profile $u_s$, its absolute value, bounded-distance differences, and open-interval time derivative are bounded, respectively, by $$\label{eq:smoothbounds} C G(z),\qquad C W(z),\qquad C W(z)/D_*.$$ For the full point profile $w_s$, put $d=\sqrt{1+s}$. The corresponding bounds are $$\label{eq:pointbounds} C P_d(z),\qquad C W_d(z),\qquad C W_d(z)/d.$$ The smooth profile is slot-uniform and does not change at the seam. The slot differences and seam changes of the point profile satisfy the difference bound in <a href="#eq:pointbounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pointbounds">[eq:pointbounds]</a>. For each fixed $a>0$, when $s\ge a\beta$ the bounds <a href="#eq:smoothbounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:smoothbounds">[eq:smoothbounds]</a> also hold for $w_s$, with a constant depending on $a$.
  - Explanation: The smooth and point profiles have uniform bounds on values, spatial differences, and time derivatives.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-signedsource)): Fix a good picture in $\mathcal G$ and one directed block $I=[u,v]$. Let $Z_s$ be an isolated obstacle strand starting above $B_{4R}(x)$ on this block, with its specified sides at operations. For either profile part, the total signed mass of the assigned obstacle sources, obtained by summing over source slots and integrating in time, is $$\label{eq:signedsource} \varphi_v(Z_v)-\varphi_u(Z_u) +\int_u^v\gamma_s(Z_s)\,ds.$$ The endpoint values include any seam assigned to the block. The same formula holds in reverse elapsed time.
  - Explanation: For an isolated strand, signed source mass telescopes to the endpoint profile difference and a cutoff contribution.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-cancellation)): On a picture in $\mathcal G$, let a strand start above $z\in B_{4R}(x)$ in a directed block of length between $L$ and $2L$. For the smooth profile part, take $\mathcal W=W$. For a point-profile part, suppose that $d=\sqrt{1+s}$ is comparable throughout the block and $\inf d\ge L$, and take $\mathcal W=W_d$ at its starting elapsed time. Then the obstacle sources assigned to this strand are supported within distance $CL$ of $z$ and have total variation at most $$\label{eq:blocksourcevariation} CL\mathcal W(z).$$ If the strand is isolated, their signed sum has absolute value at most $$\label{eq:blocksourcecancellation} CL^{3/4}\mathcal W(z).$$ Both assertions also hold with $\mathcal W=W$ for the point profile on blocks in the second half of elapsed time, and for the sum of that part and the smooth part, after changing the constant.
  - Explanation: Isolation improves signed block mass relative to the bound on total variation.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-sourcebins)): On a picture in $\mathcal G$, the obstacle sources assigned to one strand in one directed bin, starting above $z\in B_{4R}(x)$ at elapsed time $s$, have total variation at most $$\label{eq:binsourcevariation} H_\beta\bigl(W(z)+W_d(z)\bigr),\qquad d=\sqrt{1+s}.$$ The two profile parts separately satisfy this bound with their own weight. Their source sites lie within $C\log\beta$ of $z$.
  - Explanation: Short bins control total source variation even near the initial point-profile singularity.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-duhamel)): For the comparison sources just defined, $$\label{eq:doubledduhamel} \langle \mu_+,h\rangle =\int\langle s^+(ds),\bar b_s\rangle +\iint_{s\prec t} \langle T_{t,s}s^+(ds),s^-(dt)\rangle.$$ In the second integral the reverse sources are indexed by forward physical time. The order $s\prec t$ means that the forward insertion slice lies before the reverse insertion slice. Thus $T_{t,s}$ runs from after the first operation to before the later operation when both sources are atomic. There is no term pairing the forward and reverse sources of the same operation.
  - Explanation: The scalar residual pairing equals a profile pairing and a strictly ordered interaction of forward and reverse sources.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-scalar)): With the scales of <a href="#eq:scales" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scales">[eq:scales]</a>, under the interiority and pin assumptions of Section <a href="#sec:profiles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:profiles">5</a>, $$\label{eq:scalarclaim} \mathbb E_P\bigl[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G}\|\langle \mu_+,h\rangle\|\bigr] =o(\beta^{-5/2}).$$ The error is uniform in the finite cube, the deterministic pin set, and the injection slot.
  - Explanation: The expected scalar residual at the trial potential is negligible at the required correction scale.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-boxmoment)): Partition the sites into cubes $Q$ of side comparable to $F\ge1$. At any deterministic slice let $\mathcal O$ be its obstacle slots, and let $w$ be a nonnegative deterministic site weight of finite support. With slot multiplicity in the sums, set $A_Q=\sum_{(z,a)\in\mathcal O,\ z\in Q}w(z)$. Then $$\label{eq:boxmoment} \left(\mathbb E_P\sum_Q A_Q^2\right)^{1/2} \le C\left[ \sqrt\lambda\,\\|w\\|_2 +\lambda\left\{\sum_Q \left(\sum_{z\in Q}w(z)\right)^2\right\}^{1/2} \right].$$ If two such sums are taken at different deterministic slices, the expected sum of products over pairs of anchors at distance at most $CF$ is bounded by a constant times the product of their respective right sides in <a href="#eq:boxmoment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:boxmoment">[eq:boxmoment]</a>.
  - Explanation: Snapshot bounds control weighted box moments and pairings at different times without independence.
- **C21** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-dualenergy)): Fix $S$, and use the scales in <a href="#eq:scales" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scales">[eq:scales]</a>. Suppose that $B_{50R_*}(x)$ lies in the interior of the finite cube and that no point of $E$ lies above this ball. Let $\mathcal G$ be the good event of Proposition <a href="#prop:good" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:good">10</a>, and let $\mu_+,\mu_-$ be the terminal residuals in <a href="#eq:residualdefinition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:residualdefinition">[eq:residualdefinition]</a>. There are nonnegative measurable functions $X_+,X_-$ of the exposure and a deterministic rapidly small function $\rho(\beta)$ such that, on $\mathcal G$, $$\label{eq:dualenergy} \lvert \langle \mu_d,y\rangle\rvert \le X_d\sqrt{e_y}+C_0\rho(\beta), \qquad d\in\{+,-\},$$ for every real cut vector $y$ that vanishes on the cut killing slots and satisfies $\lVert y\rVert_\infty\le C_0$. The functions $X_d$ do not depend on $y$ or $C_0$, and $$\label{eq:dualenergyexpectation} \mathbb E_P\!\left[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G}(X_+^2+X_-^2)\right] =o(\beta^{-5/2}).$$ All estimates are uniform over the finite cubes, deterministic pin sets, and injection slots satisfying the stated assumptions.
  - Explanation: Both terminal residuals admit energy-dual bounds whose expected squared coefficients are negligible at the required scale.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-blockflow)): For the first collection of sources, fix a block $I_j$ on $\mathcal G$. Propagate its sources to a fully regular slice $t$ of $I_{j+1}$, interpreting $I_{n+1}=\beta+I_1$ in a copy of the period. Their propagated signed mass differs in counting $\ell^1$ norm by a rapidly small amount from a localized mass $\nu_j(t)$, supported in $B_{6R}(x)$, which is the divergence of a flow of squared norm at most $$\label{eq:blockflowbound} F_j=C\left(L^6Q_j+L^2C_j+L^2b_*^2R^5\right).$$ The constant and the localization error are uniform in $j$ and $t$. Moreover, $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:blockflowexpectation} \mathbb E_P[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G}F_j] \le C\bigl[&L^6\lambda\beta^{-2}D_*^{-1} +L^2(L^{-1/2}\lambda^2+L^3\lambda^3)\beta^{-2}R +L^2b_*^2R^5\bigr]. \end{aligned}$$ If the sources of the final block are propagated only to the terminal cut, their mass differs by a rapidly small amount in $\ell^1$ from a mass $m_{\mathrm{loc}}$ satisfying $$\label{eq:lastblocknorm} \lVert m_{\mathrm{loc}}\rVert_2^2 \le K_n:=CL^5(Q_n+b_*^2R^3).$$
  - Explanation: Localized block sources can be routed through local and infinite flows, with explicit snapshot bounds on their energy cost.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-earlysourceenergy)): Let $\mu_d^{\mathrm{early}}$ be the terminal mass of the point-profile obstacle sources at elapsed times in $[0,\beta/2)$, and let $M$ be their total variation before propagation. Then $$\label{eq:earlymassmoment} \mathbb E_P[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G}M^2]\le H_\beta\beta^{-3/2}.$$ For every test vector of Proposition <a href="#prop:dualenergy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dualenergy">21</a>, on $\mathcal G$, $$\label{eq:earlydualbound} \lvert \langle \mu_d^{\mathrm{early}},y\rangle\rvert \le CM\beta^{-3/4}\sqrt{e_y}+C_0\rho(\beta).$$
  - Explanation: Early point-profile sources diffuse before the terminal pairing, providing an energy bound without block cancellation.
- **C24** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-surfacecost)): For every finite $\beta>0$, every $t\ge0$, and every finite cube, $$\label{eq:surfacecost} 0\le p_N(t)-p_N^+(t) \le (\log2)\left(\frac\ell\beta+24\ell^2\right) \frac{\|\partial\Lambda_N\|}{V_N}.$$ In particular $p_N^+(t)\to p_{S,\beta}(t)$ for each fixed $\beta>0$ and $t\ge0$.
  - Explanation: All-time boundary pins change pressure by a surface cost that vanishes in the thermodynamic limit.

        "exact_quote": "At fixed exposure, every killed interval kernel and its adjoint are substochastic in both counting sums and contract counting $\\ell^p$ for $p=1,2,\\infty$. Equation <a href=\"#eq:energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:energy\">[eq:energy]</a> concerns one complete traversal; each copy of that traversal has the same bound.",

Between instantaneous operations a propagated vector satisfies $$\\frac{d}{dt}\\lVert v_t\\rVert_2^2=-2\\mathcal D_t(v_t).$$ The squared norm cannot increase at an instantaneous operation.*\n\n\n*Let $U$ be a one-period kernel with any allowed pin killings, and let $y$ vanish on its cut killing slots. Set $e_y=\\langle y,(I-U)y\\rangle\\ge0$. For either $U_d=U$ or $U^*$, let $Y_t$ be the adjoint propagation of $y$ from the terminal cut to an intermediate slice. Then $$\\label{eq:energy}\n \\int_{\\mathrm{traversal}}\\mathcal D_t(Y_t)\\,dt\\le e_y,\n \\qquad\n \\lVert y-U_d^*y\\rVert_2^2\\le2e_y.$$ The contraction and local norm-loss statements hold with extra spatial killing and for intervals continued across a period cut. Equation <a href=\"#eq:energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:energy\">[eq:energy]</a> concerns one complete traversal; each copy of that traversal has the same bound.",

      "exact_statement": "At fixed exposure, every killed interval kernel and its adjoint are substochastic in both counting sums and contract counting $\\ell^p$ for $p=1,2,\\infty$. Set $e_y=\\langle y,(I-U)y\\rangle\\ge0$. For either $U_d=U$ or $U^*$, let $Y_t$ be the adjoint propagation of $y$ from the terminal cut to an intermediate slice. Then $$\\label{eq:energy}\n \\int_{\\mathrm{traversal}}\\mathcal D_t(Y_t)\\,dt\\le e_y,\n \\qquad\n \\lVert y-U_d^*y\\rVert_2^2\\le2e_y.$$ The contraction and local norm-loss statements hold with extra spatial killing and for intervals continued across a period cut.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed positive half-integer $S$ there are $\\beta_0(S)<\\infty$ and a function $\\varepsilon_S(\\beta)\\to0$ as $\\beta\\to\\infty$ such that the following holds for every $\\beta\\ge\\beta_0(S)$. Let $\\Lambda$ be any finite cube, let $E=D_0\\cup F$ with $F$ any finite deterministic set of slot-time pins, and let $i=(x,a)$ be a time-zero post-seam slot. Suppose that $B_{50R_*}(x)$ is interior to $\\Lambda$ and that $E$ has no point above this ball. Then $$\\label{eq:pinnedcomparison}\n \\lvert d_E(i)-\\frac 1\\ell n_S^{\\rm sw}(\\beta)\\rvert\n \\le \\varepsilon_S(\\beta)\\beta^{-5/2}.$$ The same uniform error bound, after enlarging $\\varepsilon_S$ if necessary, holds for $\\lvert \\mathbb E_{E\\cup\\{i\\}}q_i-\\ell^{-1}n_S^{\\rm sw}(\\beta)\\rvert$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-capacity",
        "exact_quote": "For the preceding data, put $y=f-h$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n A^*f&=(1-q)\\eta,                                      \\label{eq:harmonic}\\\\\n e_y&=\\langle y,\\mu_-\\rangle,                                  \\label{eq:errorenergy}\\\\\n q-(1-c)\n &=\\langle h,\\mu_+\\rangle+\\langle y,\\mu_+\\rangle.                     \\label{eq:capacityidentity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ No assumption of almost-sure killing is required.*\n\n\n\n*Suppose, in addition, that $\\lVert h\\rVert_\\infty\\le M$ and there are numbers $X,\\varepsilon\\ge0$ such that, for both $d=+,-$, $$\\label{eq:abstractdual}\n \\lvert \\langle \\mu_d,z\\rangle\\rvert\\le X\\sqrt{e_z}+\\varepsilon\n \\quad\\text{whenever }z|_K=0\\text{ and }\\lVert z\\rVert_\\infty\\le1+M.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:capacitystability}\n \\lvert q-(1-c)\\rvert\n \\le \\lvert \\langle h,\\mu_+\\rangle\\rvert+2X^2+2\\varepsilon.$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-geometryinputs",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $\\beta$, every allowed pin set $D$, every deterministic time, every anchor $x$, and every $R_0$ above a fixed threshold, the following bounds hold. Put $p=(5/\\beta)^{10/11}$.*\n\n\n1.  *The probability of a nonregular time under $\\mathbb P_D$ is at most $$\\label{eq:regularityprob}\n     C R_0^3p^6+C e^{-cR_0^\\xi},$$ for some fixed $\\xi>0$. Failure of the second regularity condition alone has probability at most $C e^{-cR_0^\\xi}$.*\n\n\n2.  *At every locally regular time, every nonnegative vector on the hole graph supported above $B_{R_0}(x)$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:nash}\n     \\lVert v\\rVert_2^{2+4/3}\\le C\\mathcal D_t(v)\\lVert v\\rVert_1^{4/3}.$$ There is an available slot within bounded distance of every site in $B_{2R_0}(x)$. Available slots over two sites in this ball at distance $b$ can be joined by a path of length at most $C(1+b)$, staying within distance $C(1+b)$ of the first site and inside $B_{3R_0}(x)$.*\n\n\n3.  *At every regular time, each available slot $a$ above $B_{R_0}(x)$ admits a unit flow $J_a$ to infinity on the extended hole graph. For $b$ the distance between its source site and an edge, $$\\label{eq:flowenvelopes}\n     |J_a(e)|\\le C(1+b)^{-199/100},\n     \\qquad |J_a(e)|\\le C(1+b)^{-2}\\quad(b\\le R_0).$$*\n\n*All constants are uniform in the volume, pins, anchor, and deterministic time.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.2"
      },

Available slots over two sites in this ball at distance $b$ can be joined by a path of length at most $C(1+b)$, staying within distance $C(1+b)$ of the first site and inside $B_{3R_0}(x)$.*\n\n\n3.  *At every regular time, each available slot $a$ above $B_{R_0}(x)$ admits a unit flow $J_a$ to infinity on the extended hole graph.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-isolatedpath",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an allowed pin set $P$, a deterministic slot $v$, and an interval $[s,t]$ of positive length less than $\\beta$ containing no seam in its interior. Follow the picture’s strand $X$ from $v$ at time $s$ to time $t$. Let $\\mathcal I$ be the event that this strand is exposed and, throughout the interval, no other exposed slot lies at its site or a neighboring site, on both sides of every jump. For every measurable path event $\\mathcal A$ consisting of paths that stay in the interior, $$\\label{eq:isolateddomination}\n \\mathbb P_P\\bigl(\\mathcal I\\cap\\{X\\in\\mathcal A\\}\\bigr)\n       \\le2\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}\\mathbf 1_{\\{X\\in\\mathcal A\\}}.$$ On the right, $X$ is the base tagged walk, with rate $1/2$ along each slot edge; on interior paths its law is the free walk law. Any seam at an endpoint is excluded: use the post-seam side at $s$ and the pre-seam side at $t$. The same bound holds when both paths are traced backward from a prescribed slot at $t$ to $s$, with these same chronological sides.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-isolatedpath",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an allowed pin set $P$, a deterministic slot $v$, and an interval $[s,t]$ of positive length less than $\\beta$ containing no seam in its interior. Let $\\mathcal I$ be the event that this strand is exposed and, throughout the interval, no other exposed slot lies at its site or a neighboring site, on both sides of every jump. For every measurable path event $\\mathcal A$ consisting of paths that stay in the interior, $$\\label{eq:isolateddomination}\n \\mathbb P_P\\bigl(\\mathcal I\\cap\\{X\\in\\mathcal A\\}\\bigr)\n       \\le2\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}\\mathbf 1_{\\{X\\in\\mathcal A\\}}.$$ On the right, $X$ is the base tagged walk, with rate $1/2$ along each slot edge; on interior paths its law is the free walk law. Any seam at an endpoint is excluded: use the post-seam side at $s$ and the pre-seam side at $t$. The same bound holds when both paths are traced backward from a prescribed slot at $t$ to $s$, with these same chronological sides.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-good",
        "exact_quote": "Under $\\mathbb P_P$ there is an event $\\mathcal G$ with $$\\label{eq:goodprob}\n \\mathbb P_P(\\mathcal G^c)=o(\\beta^{-5/2})$$ having the following properties in $B_{12R_*}(x)$, in either direction of traversal.*\n\n1.  *A strand makes at most $c_0L$ inter-site steps in a block and at most $C\\log\\beta$ in a bin. The bounds also apply to segments traced from an intermediate visit to the region, in either time direction.*\n\n2.  *At each block cut, every ball of radius $c_1L$ centered in the region contains at most five obstacle slots. Local regularity at radius $R_*$ about $x$ holds at all times. In every block, the set of fully regular times at that radius has length at least half the block.*\n\n3.  *A strand is called isolated in a block and direction if no other obstacle slot lies within $c_2L$ of its starting site. Every such strand stays at distance greater than two from all other obstacles and has maximal site displacement at most $CL^{3/4}$ in the block.*\n\n*The constants are fixed in the order $c_0$, then $c_2$, then $c_1$, with each sufficiently large for the preceding choices. The event and all its properties repeat on subsequent copies of the period.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-good",
          "exact_quote": "Under $\\mathbb P_P$ there is an event $\\mathcal G$ with $$\\label{eq:goodprob}\n \\mathbb P_P(\\mathcal G^c)=o(\\beta^{-5/2})$$ having the following properties in $B_{12R_*}(x)$, in either direction of traversal.*\n\n1.  *A strand makes at most $c_0L$ inter-site steps in a block and at most $C\\log\\beta$ in a bin. The bounds also apply to segments traced from an intermediate visit to the region, in either time direction.*\n\n2.  *At each block cut, every ball of radius $c_1L$ centered in the region contains at most five obstacle slots. Local regularity at radius $R_*$ about $x$ holds at all times. In every block, the set of fully regular times at that radius has length at least half the block.*\n\n3.  *A strand is called isolated in a block and direction if no other obstacle slot lies within $c_2L$ of its starting site. Every such strand stays at distance greater than two from all other obstacles and has maximal site displacement at most $CL^{3/4}$ in the block.*\n\n*The constants are fixed in the order $c_0$, then $c_2$, then $c_1$, with each sufficiently large for the preceding choices. The event and all its properties repeat on subsequent copies of the period.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Under $\\mathbb P_P$ there is an event $\\mathcal G$ with $$\\label{eq:goodprob}\n \\mathbb P_P(\\mathcal G^c)=o(\\beta^{-5/2})$$ having the following properties in $B_{12R_*}(x)$, in either direction of traversal.*\n\n1.  *A strand makes at most $c_0L$ inter-site steps in a block and at most $C\\log\\beta$ in a bin. The bounds also apply to segments traced from an intermediate visit to the region, in either time direction.*\n\n2.  *At each block cut, every ball of radius $c_1L$ centered in the region contains at most five obstacle slots. Local regularity at radius $R_*$ about $x$ holds at all times. In every block, the set of fully regular times at that radius has length at least half the block.*\n\n3.  *A strand is called isolated in a block and direction if no other obstacle slot lies within $c_2L$ of its starting site. Every such strand stays at distance greater than two from all other obstacles and has maximal site displacement at most $CL^{3/4}$ in the block.*\n\n*The constants are fixed in the order $c_0$, then $c_2$, then $c_1$, with each sufficiently large for the preceding choices.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-freebounds",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed $S$ and all sufficiently large $\\beta$, the following bounds hold uniformly in the direction, elapsed time, site, and slot. For the full smooth profile $u_s$, its absolute value, bounded-distance differences, and open-interval time derivative are bounded, respectively, by $$\\label{eq:smoothbounds}\n C G(z),\\qquad C W(z),\\qquad C W(z)/D_*.$$ For the full point profile $w_s$, put $d=\\sqrt{1+s}$. The corresponding bounds are $$\\label{eq:pointbounds}\n C P_d(z),\\qquad C W_d(z),\\qquad C W_d(z)/d.$$ The smooth profile is slot-uniform and does not change at the seam. The slot differences and seam changes of the point profile satisfy the difference bound in <a href=\"#eq:pointbounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pointbounds\">[eq:pointbounds]</a>. For each fixed $a>0$, when $s\\ge a\\beta$ the bounds <a href=\"#eq:smoothbounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:smoothbounds\">[eq:smoothbounds]</a> also hold for $w_s$, with a constant depending on $a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-signedsource",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a good picture in $\\mathcal G$ and one directed block $I=[u,v]$. Let $Z_s$ be an isolated obstacle strand starting above $B_{4R}(x)$ on this block, with its specified sides at operations.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-cancellation",
        "exact_quote": "On a picture in $\\mathcal G$, let a strand start above $z\\in B_{4R}(x)$ in a directed block of length between $L$ and $2L$. For the smooth profile part, take $\\mathcal W=W$. For a point-profile part, suppose that $d=\\sqrt{1+s}$ is comparable throughout the block and $\\inf d\\ge L$, and take $\\mathcal W=W_d$ at its starting elapsed time. Then the obstacle sources assigned to this strand are supported within distance $CL$ of $z$ and have total variation at most $$\\label{eq:blocksourcevariation}\n CL\\mathcal W(z).$$ If the strand is isolated, their signed sum has absolute value at most $$\\label{eq:blocksourcecancellation}\n CL^{3/4}\\mathcal W(z).$$ Both assertions also hold with $\\mathcal W=W$ for the point profile on blocks in the second half of elapsed time, and for the sum of that part and the smooth part, after changing the constant.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-duhamel",
        "exact_quote": "For the comparison sources just defined, $$\\label{eq:doubledduhamel}\n \\langle \\mu_+,h\\rangle\n =\\int\\langle s^+(ds),\\bar b_s\\rangle\n  +\\iint_{s\\prec t}\n          \\langle T_{t,s}s^+(ds),s^-(dt)\\rangle.$$ In the second integral the reverse sources are indexed by forward physical time. The order $s\\prec t$ means that the forward insertion slice lies before the reverse insertion slice. Thus $T_{t,s}$ runs from after the first operation to before the later operation when both sources are atomic. There is no term pairing the forward and reverse sources of the same operation.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-271.2"
        }

        "exact_quote": "Partition the sites into cubes $Q$ of side comparable to $F\\ge1$. At any deterministic slice let $\\mathcal O$ be its obstacle slots, and let $w$ be a nonnegative deterministic site weight of finite support. With slot multiplicity in the sums, set $A_Q=\\sum_{(z,a)\\in\\mathcal O,\\ z\\in Q}w(z)$. Then $$\\label{eq:boxmoment}\n \\left(\\mathbb E_P\\sum_Q A_Q^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le C\\left[\n  \\sqrt\\lambda\\,\\|w\\|_2\n  +\\lambda\\left\\{\\sum_Q\n                     \\left(\\sum_{z\\in Q}w(z)\\right)^2\\right\\}^{1/2}\n                 \\right].$$ If two such sums are taken at different deterministic slices, the expected sum of products over pairs of anchors at distance at most $CF$ is bounded by a constant times the product of their respective right sides in <a href=\"#eq:boxmoment\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:boxmoment\">[eq:boxmoment]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-271.2"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Spin operators** ([source](#pm-h-3)): At each site $x$ let $\boldsymbol S_x=(S_x^1,S_x^2,S_x^3)$ be the spin-$S$ operators on $\mathbb C^{2S+1}$.
- **Hamiltonian and total spin** ([source](#pm-h-3)): With free boundary conditions, the Hamiltonian and total $z$-spin are $$H_{S,N}=-\sum_{\substack{\{x,y\}\subset\Lambda_N\\ \|x-y\|_1=1}} \boldsymbol S_x\cdot\boldsymbol S_y, \qquad M_N=\sum_{x\in\Lambda_N}S_x^3.$$
- **Pressure and spontaneous magnetization** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For inverse temperature $\beta>0$ and field $h\ge0$, define $$p_{S,\beta}(h) =\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1{\beta\|\Lambda_N\|} \log\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-\beta(H_{S,N}-hM_N)}, \qquad m_S(\beta)=\partial_{h+}p_{S,\beta}(0).$$
- **Ideal-magnon density** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The full ideal-magnon density uses the lattice dispersion $\varepsilon_S(k)=2S\sum_{j=1}^3(1-\cos k_j)$: $$\label{eq:ideal-density} n_S^{\mathrm{sw}}(\beta) =\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^3} \frac{d^3k}{(2\pi)^3\,[e^{\beta\varepsilon_S(k)}-1]}.$$
- **Allowed pins** ([source](#pm-h-7)): An *allowed pin set* is $D=D_0\cup F$, where $F$ is a finite deterministic set of slot-time points.
- **Dirichlet energy** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a vector extended by zero on the inner boundary and outside $\Lambda$, define $$\label{eq:dirichlet} \mathcal D_t(v)=\frac12\sum_{\{a,b\}\text{ available at }t} (v(a)-v(b))^2.$$
- **Capacity residuals** ([source](#sec:capacity)): Choose a real vector $h$ with $h_i=1$ and $h=0$ on $K$, and a real number $c$. Define its two residuals by $$\label{eq:capacityresiduals} \mu_+=Uh-h+c\eta,\qquad \mu_-=U^*h-h+c\eta.$$
- **Free trial vector** ([source](#pm-h-17)): The full trial vector is $$\label{eq:trialvector} h=c\chi(\eta+v),$$ and the same symbol denotes its restriction to the cut holes.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **Rloops** [claim](#stmt-prop-loopinputs) / [proof](#sec:loops): Cycle identities and contraction energy -> Pinned down probabilities expressed through fresh-walk returns
- **Rcapacity** [claim](#stmt-lem-capacity) / [proof](#sec:capacity): Finite-dimensional hitting identities -> Capacity stability from scalar and energy residuals
- **Rgeometry** [claim](#stmt-prop-good) / [proof](#sec:geometry): Snapshot sparsity and isolated-path domination -> Good exposures and localized propagation bounds
- **Rprofiles** [claim](#stmt-lem-freebounds) / [proof](#sec:profiles): All-turn heat-kernel potential and spatial cutoff -> Forward and reverse trial profiles
- **Rsources** [claim](#stmt-lem-duhamel) / [proof](#sec:sources): Signed conservation and ordered Duhamel telescoping -> Local comparison sources and isolated-strand cancellation
- **Rscalar** [claim](#stmt-prop-scalar) / [proof](#sec:scalar): Snapshot moments and ordered source pairing -> Negligible expected scalar residual
- **Renergy** [claim](#stmt-prop-dualenergy) / [proof](#sec:energy): Divergence flows and successor-block averaging -> Negligible expected squared dual-energy coefficients
- **Rcomparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-comparison) / [proof](#pm-h-35): Averaged capacity criterion -> Uniform pinned comparison with ideal density
- **Rpressure** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-37): Field-pin mixture and pressure difference quotients -> Thermodynamic spontaneous magnetization asymptotic
- **Rexpansion** [claim](#stmt-cor-expansion) / [proof](#pm-h-38): Gaussian expansion of the lattice heat kernel -> Explicit first lattice correction

          "exact_quote": "The following statements hold at every finite volume and $\\beta>0$.*\n\n1.  *If $q_\\Lambda$ is the number of slot edges and $c(\\omega)$ the number of cycles, then $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-\\beta H_{S,\\Lambda}}\n           =e^{\\beta q_\\Lambda/4}\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm b}2^{c(\\omega)}.$$ The spectral distribution of the total $z$-spin is obtained by the unpinned color law, assigning $+1/2$ to each up slot and $-1/2$ to each down slot at time zero. The expectation, in this normalized color law, of the exponential of $\\beta$ times the field times this sum is the ratio of the field and zero-field partition functions.*\n\n\n2.  *For the allowed sets $E,P$ and point $i$ above, $$\\label{eq:pinreturn}\n     \\mathbb P_E(i\\in\\mathcal B_E)=\\frac{2r}{1+r},\n     \\qquad d_E(i)=\\frac{r}{1+r},\\qquad r=\\mathbb E_Pq_i.$$*\n\n\n3.  *More generally, let $T$ be a nonempty finite set of points at one deterministic time, disjoint from an allowed $D$, and put $P'=D\\cup T$. Expose $\\mathcal B_{P'}$ and let $q_j^{D,T}$ be the fresh-walk probability from $j\\in T$ of hitting $T$ at positive elapsed time before $D$. Then $$\\label{eq:simultaneouspin}\n     \\mathbb P_D(T\\subset\\mathcal B_D)\\le (2r_T)^{|T|},\n     \\qquad\n     r_T=\\frac1{|T|}\\sum_{j\\in T}\\mathbb E_{P'}q_j^{D,T}.$$*\n\n4.  *For any allowed $D$, the factorial measure of $j$ distinct inter-site marks under $\\mathbb P_D$ is bounded by $2^j$ times the base factorial measure. This holds with chronological ordering and with arbitrary deterministic restrictions on the edges and times.",
          "source_id": "PM-271.2"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **capacity-identities**: The hitting potential satisfies the adjoint harmonic equation; its difference from the trial potential gives the error-energy and capacity identities. Almost-sure killing is unnecessary.
- **fixed-spin-limits**: The magnetization deficit agrees with the full ideal-magnon density at the stated low-temperature precision for fixed positive half-integer spin.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **transpose-orientation**: Treat the period transition as symmetric when computing hitting probabilities. -> Forward and adjoint residuals are interchanged and the capacity calculation can fail.
- **finite-to-asymptotic**: Use finite matrix checks as a certificate of the magnetization expansion. -> Finite identities alone leave the uniform residual estimates and thermodynamic passage unchecked.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite capacity comparison gives exact identities for a finite doubly substochastic matrix and a trial potential. The implementation computes these identities; it does not decide the thermodynamic or low-temperature limits.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of rational column-transition matrices, target i, killed indices K, trial h and c.
# PRECONDITIONS: finite nonnegative doubly substochastic U; zero rows and columns on K; i outside K; h_i=1 and h_K=0.
# OUTPUTS: exact hitting vector, positive-step return probability, error energy and capacity residual pairings.
# TIME: cubic rational elimination per matrix; bit cost depends on rational input lengths.
# SPACE: quadratic rational storage per matrix.
# ARITHMETIC: exact python-flint fmpq; no floating point.
# FAILURE: invalid precondition or singular reachable subsystem raises an exception.
import sys,json
from flint import fmpq as Q

def calc(d):
    U=[[Q(v) for v in row] for row in d['U']]
    n=len(U); i=d['i']; K=set(d['K']); h=list(map(Q,d['h'])); c=Q(d['c'])
    assert n and all(len(row)==n for row in U) and len(h)==n and 0<=i<n and i not in K
    assert all(0<=k<n for k in K) and h[i]==1 and all(h[k]==0 for k in K)
    assert all(v>=0 for row in U for v in row)
    assert all(sum(row)<=1 for row in U) and all(sum(U[a][b] for a in range(n))<=1 for b in range(n))
    assert all(U[k][a]==0 and U[a][k]==0 for k in K for a in range(n))
    reach={i}
    for _ in range(n):
        reach |= {j for j in range(n) if any(U[k][j]>0 for k in reach)}
    active=sorted(reach-{i}-K)
    aug=[[Q(a==b)-U[b][a] for b in active]+[U[i][a]] for a in active]
    for t in range(len(active)):
        p=next(r for r in range(t,len(active)) if aug[r][t])
        aug[t],aug[p]=aug[p],aug[t]
        divisor=aug[t][t]; aug[t]=[v/divisor for v in aug[t]]
        for r in range(len(active)):
            if r!=t:
                factor=aug[r][t]; aug[r]=[a-factor*b for a,b in zip(aug[r],aug[t])]
    f=[Q(0) for _ in range(n)]; f[i]=Q(1)
    for t,a in enumerate(active): f[a]=aug[t][-1]
    q=sum(U[k][i]*f[k] for k in range(n))
    mu_plus=[sum(U[a][b]*h[b] for b in range(n))-h[a]+c*Q(a==i) for a in range(n)]
    mu_minus=[sum(U[b][a]*h[b] for b in range(n))-h[a]+c*Q(a==i) for a in range(n)]
    y=[f[a]-h[a] for a in range(n)]
    energy=sum(y[a]*(y[a]-sum(U[a][b]*y[b] for b in range(n))) for a in range(n))
    err=sum(y[a]*mu_minus[a] for a in range(n))
    pair=sum(f[a]*mu_plus[a] for a in range(n))
    return dict(f=list(map(str,f)),q=str(q),energy=str(energy),error_pair=str(err),capacity_lhs=str(q-(1-c)),capacity_rhs=str(pair))
print(json.dumps([calc(d) for d in json.load(sys.stdin)],sort_keys=True))
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys,json\nimport sympy as sp\ncases=json.load(open(sys.argv[1])); result=json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))\nassert sys.argv[3]=='VALID_CAPACITY' and len(cases)==len(result)\nt=sp.Symbol('t',positive=True)\nfor d,o in zip(cases,result):\n    B=sp.Matrix([[sp.Rational(x) for x in row] for row in d['U']]); m=B.rows; target=d['i']\n    assert B.cols==m and all(x>=0 for x in B)\n    assert all(sum(B.row(k))<=1 and sum(B.col(k))<=1 for k in range(m))\n    killed=d['K']; trial=sp.Matrix([sp.Rational(x) for x in d['h']]); scalar=sp.Rational(d['c'])\n    assert target not in killed and trial[target]==1\n    assert all(trial[k]==0 and all(B[k,j]==0 and B[j,k]==0 for j in range(m)) for k in killed)\n    rest=[k for k in range(m) if k!=target]\n    block=B.extract(rest,rest).T; rhs=sp.Matrix([B[target,k] for k in rest])\n    discounted=(sp.eye(len(rest))-t*block).inv()*(t*rhs)\n    hit=sp.zeros(m,1); hit[target]=1\n    for k,expression in zip(rest,discounted): hit[k]=sp.limit(expression,t,1,dir='-')\n    probability=(B.T*hit)[target]\n    assert sp.Matrix([sp.Rational(x) for x in o['f']])==hit and sp.Rational(o['q'])==probability\n    A=sp.eye(m)-B; eta=sp.eye(m)[:,target]; delta=hit-trial\n    left=(delta.T*A*delta)[0]; adj=(delta.T*(-A.T*trial+scalar*eta))[0]\n    cap=(hit.T*(-A*trial+scalar*eta))[0]\n    assert left>=0 and left==adj and probability-(1-scalar)==cap\n    assert sp.Rational(o['energy'])==left and sp.Rational(o['error_pair'])==adj\n    assert sp.Rational(o['capacity_lhs'])==probability-(1-scalar) and sp.Rational(o['capacity_rhs'])==cap\n",
    "output_contract": "Exact hitting probabilities and the error-energy and capacity pairings for the supplied finite matrix.",
    "reason": "The finite capacity comparison gives exact identities for a finite doubly substochastic matrix and a trial potential. The implementation computes these identities; it does not decide the thermodynamic or low-temperature limits.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of rational column-transition matrices, target i, killed indices K, trial h and c.\n# PRECONDITIONS: finite nonnegative doubly substochastic U; zero rows and columns on K; i outside K; h_i=1 and h_K=0.\n# OUTPUTS: exact hitting vector, positive-step return probability, error energy and capacity residual pairings.\n# TIME: cubic rational elimination per matrix; bit cost depends on rational input lengths.\n# SPACE: quadratic rational storage per matrix.\n# ARITHMETIC: exact python-flint fmpq; no floating point.\n# FAILURE: invalid precondition or singular reachable subsystem raises an exception.\nimport sys,json\nfrom flint import fmpq as Q\n\ndef calc(d):\n    U=[[Q(v) for v in row] for row in d['U']]\n    n=len(U); i=d['i']; K=set(d['K']); h=list(map(Q,d['h'])); c=Q(d['c'])\n    assert n and all(len(row)==n for row in U) and len(h)==n and 0<=i<n and i not in K\n    assert all(0<=k<n for k in K) and h[i]==1 and all(h[k]==0 for k in K)\n    assert all(v>=0 for row in U for v in row)\n    assert all(sum(row)<=1 for row in U) and all(sum(U[a][b] for a in range(n))<=1 for b in range(n))\n    assert all(U[k][a]==0 and U[a][k]==0 for k in K for a in range(n))\n    reach={i}\n    for _ in range(n):\n        reach |= {j for j in range(n) if any(U[k][j]>0 for k in reach)}\n    active=sorted(reach-{i}-K)\n    aug=[[Q(a==b)-U[b][a] for b in active]+[U[i][a]] for a in active]\n    for t in range(len(active)):\n        p=next(r for r in range(t,len(active)) if aug[r][t])\n        aug[t],aug[p]=aug[p],aug[t]\n        divisor=aug[t][t]; aug[t]=[v/divisor for v in aug[t]]\n        for r in range(len(active)):\n            if r!=t:\n                factor=aug[r][t]; aug[r]=[a-factor*b for a,b in zip(aug[r],aug[t])]\n    f=[Q(0) for _ in range(n)]; f[i]=Q(1)\n    for t,a in enumerate(active): f[a]=aug[t][-1]\n    q=sum(U[k][i]*f[k] for k in range(n))\n    mu_plus=[sum(U[a][b]*h[b] for b in range(n))-h[a]+c*Q(a==i) for a in range(n)]\n    mu_minus=[sum(U[b][a]*h[b] for b in range(n))-h[a]+c*Q(a==i) for a in range(n)]\n    y=[f[a]-h[a] for a in range(n)]\n    energy=sum(y[a]*(y[a]-sum(U[a][b]*y[b] for b in range(n))) for a in range(n))\n    err=sum(y[a]*mu_minus[a] for a in range(n))\n    pair=sum(f[a]*mu_plus[a] for a in range(n))\n    return dict(f=list(map(str,f)),q=str(q),energy=str(energy),error_pair=str(err),capacity_lhs=str(q-(1-c)),capacity_rhs=str(pair))\nprint(json.dumps([calc(d) for d in json.load(sys.stdin)],sort_keys=True))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite capacity identities provide conditional consistency checks for software representing killed Markov kernels. A computational implementation is an inferred use of the mathematical criterion. |
| games_simulation | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A simulation of the stated exchange-cycle model can use the fresh-walk definition and counting-norm contractions as consistency conditions. Transfer to game dynamics is not established. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A mathematical checking tool can compare finite capacity residual identities and kernel contractions under the stated hypotheses. The paper supplies mathematical identities rather than a software tool. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The capacity comparison can serve as a conditional residual criterion for a computed hitting potential. Construction of a solver and its computational complexity are not supplied. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coordinate sets and matrices describe mathematical objects; no storage representation, indexing method or data-structure performance result is established. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Counting-norm contractions concern mathematical kernels and do not establish an accelerator design or a hardware performance contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The return-probability and magnetization results do not establish a cryptographic construction or a security property. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The residual comparison is a potential-theoretic estimate; the source gives no learning objective, training procedure or prediction guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The flows and energy pairings are proof estimates. They do not specify an optimisation objective, a scheduling problem or an operations-research solver. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Period composition and ordered propagation are features of the exposed walk; they do not establish a distributed protocol or a consensus guarantee. |
| other (Quantum lattice statistical mechanics) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The paper directly establishes the first lattice correction to spontaneous magnetization for the stated nearest-neighbor quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet at fixed spin. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The magnetization result concerns the three-dimensional nearest-neighbor quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet with the stated Hamiltonian and free boundary conditions. Blocked use: ['Transfer to a different lattice, interaction, or physical model without an additional theorem.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): Spin is a fixed positive half-integer. Constants may depend on spin; the result does not supply uniform control in a varying-spin limit. Blocked use: ['Taking spin to infinity together with the low-temperature limit.', 'Applying the theorem outside the stated spin domain.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): Spontaneous magnetization is the right field derivative of the thermodynamic pressure, with the volume limit taken first and the low-temperature limit taken last. Blocked use: ['Interchanging the volume limit and the zero-field derivative.', 'Replacing the thermodynamic derivative by a finite-volume zero-field observable.']
- **L4** (approximation): The magnetization expansion is asymptotic at low temperature. It does not state an exact finite-temperature equality or a numerical threshold with an explicit error constant. Blocked use: ['Treating the displayed expansion as an exact formula at finite inverse temperature.', 'Inferring a certified numerical error bar from the little-oh remainder alone.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): Uniform pinned comparison requires the prescribed interior ball and the absence of pins above that ball. Uniformity over distant pins does not remove these local conditions. Blocked use: ['Using the local comparison near boundary pins.', 'Using the local comparison when extra pins lie in its protected ball.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): Local heat and displacement bounds are conditional on the good exposure and retain their spatial killing, starting-region and time-horizon assumptions. Blocked use: ['Applying the local bounds to an arbitrary obstacle environment.', 'Using a conditional estimate as an unconditional guarantee.']
- **L7** (approximation): The complement of the good exposure is bounded by little-oh at the stated comparison scale; it is not asserted to be rapidly small. Blocked use: ['Assigning the good-event complement the stronger decay reserved for rapidly small propagation errors.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): Isolated-strand domination controls the joint event of isolation and the path event. It does not identify the path law conditioned on isolation with a free-walk law. Blocked use: ['Replacing the joint-event bound by a bound for the conditional law without accounting for the probability of isolation.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The fresh walk uses a periodic exposed environment and resamples transition randomness on each turn. Following unexposed trajectories in a single conditioned picture gives a different object. Blocked use: ['Substituting a single conditioned exchange trajectory for the fresh Markov walk.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The finite capacity criterion requires a nonnegative doubly substochastic matrix, killing rows and columns, the forward-on-columns convention, and a normalized trial potential. Its hitting equation uses the adjoint. Blocked use: ['Applying the identities to a matrix without the stated contraction and killing conditions.', 'Replacing the adjoint hitting equation by the forward equation for a nonsymmetric matrix.']
- **L11** (edge_case): Almost-sure killing is not a hypothesis of the capacity identities. A surviving class disjoint from the target must retain its hitting-probability interpretation. Blocked use: ['Assuming an invertible global resolvent merely from the capacity hypotheses.', 'Discarding nonkilled classes when defining the hitting vector.']
- **L12** (edge_case): The uncut free potential need not be square summable. The finite-volume trial vector and restricted profiles use finite support, while uncut evolution is defined pointwise. Blocked use: ['Using a square-summable interpretation of the uncut potential without establishing it.', 'Removing the spatial cutoff from finite-volume energy pairings without justification.']
- **L13** (hypothesis): Snapshot sparsity concerns distinct slot points at a single deterministic time. Correlations between different times are controlled by moment bounds rather than an independence assertion. Blocked use: ['Multiplying snapshot probabilities across different times as if the snapshots were independent.']
- **L14** (hypothesis): The field-pin marginal is generally not Bernoulli after weighting by the pinned partition function. Independent selections are available after conditioning on the picture and colors. Blocked use: ['Treating the posterior pin set as an unweighted independent Bernoulli set.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the first lattice correction to Bloch's law for the three-dimensional nearest-neighbor quantum Heisenberg ferromagnet at every fixed spin $`S=\tfrac12,1,\tfrac32,\ldots`$. The spontaneous-magnetization deficit agrees with the full ideal-magnon density up to $`o(\beta^{-5/2})`$. In addition to the leading Bloch term, this gives the correction $`3\zeta(5/2)(\beta S)^{-5/2}/(128\pi^{3/2})`$. The magnetization is the right derivative at zero field of the thermodynamic pressure; the volume limit precedes the field derivative, and the low-temperature limit is taken last.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | For every fixed $S\in\{\tfrac12,1,\tfrac32,\ldots\}$, |
| [stmt-cor-expansion](#stmt-cor-expansion) | corollary | `cor:expansion` | 2 | For every fixed positive half-integer $S$, |
| [stmt-prop-loopinputs](#stmt-prop-loopinputs) | proposition | `prop:loopinputs` | 3 | The following statements hold at every finite volume and $\beta>0$.* |
| [stmt-lem-kernelenergy](#stmt-lem-kernelenergy) | lemma | `lem:kernelenergy` | 4 | At fixed exposure, every killed interval kernel and its adjoint are substochastic in both counting sums and contract counting $\ell^p$ for $p=1,2,\infty$. |
| [stmt-prop-comparison](#stmt-prop-comparison) | proposition | `prop:comparison` | 5 | For every fixed positive half-integer $S$ there are $\beta_0(S)<\infty$ and a function $\varepsilon_S(\beta)\to0$ as $\beta\to\infty$ such that the following holds for every $\beta\ge\beta_0(S)$. |
| [stmt-lem-capacity](#stmt-lem-capacity) | lemma | `lem:capacity` | 6 | For the preceding data, put $y=f-h$. |
| [stmt-prop-geometryinputs](#stmt-prop-geometryinputs) | proposition | `prop:geometryinputs` | 7 | For all sufficiently large $\beta$, every allowed pin set $D$, every deterministic time, every anchor $x$, and every $R_0$ above a fixed threshold, the following bounds hold. |
| [stmt-prop-snapshot](#stmt-prop-snapshot) | proposition | `prop:snapshot` | 8 | For every allowed pin set $D$ and every set $T$ of distinct slot points at one deterministic time, with $1\le &#124;T&#124;\le6$, |
| [stmt-lem-isolatedpath](#stmt-lem-isolatedpath) | lemma | `lem:isolatedpath` | 9 | Fix an allowed pin set $P$, a deterministic slot $v$, and an interval $[s,t]$ of positive length less than $\beta$ containing no seam in its interior. |
