Visual witness description of PDF page 31 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
Timeline diagram along a horizontal time axis $t$ pointing right, showing coordinates $0$ and $\beta$. Contiguous rectangular blocks labeled $I_1$, $\dots$, $I_j$, $I_{j+1}$, $\dots$, $I_n$, $I_1 + \beta$, $\dots$. A green curved arrow arches from block $I_j$ to block $I_{j+1}$ labeled "propagate, then average on regular slices". A vertical dashed line with an upward arrow at $t = \beta$ (boundary between $I_n$ and $I_1 + \beta$) labeled "continue through the next period". Below the blocks, a centered identity is displayed: $\langle m, y \rangle = \langle m, y - U_d^* y \rangle + \langle U_d m, y \rangle$.
```


<a id="eq:blockaverage"></a>
<a id="eq:smoothdualcoefficient"></a>
For each $j$, average over the fully regular subset of its successor block. Proposition <a href="#prop:good" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:good">10</a> gives this subset measure at least $cL$. Applying Cauchy–Schwarz in that time variable bounds its averaged localized pairing by $$\label{eq:blockaverage}
 C\sqrt{F_j/L}
 \left(\int_{I_{j+1}}\mathcal D_t(Y_j(t))\,dt\right)^{1/2},$$ where $Y_j(t)=T_{\beta,t}^*y$ for $j<n$, whereas $Y_n(t)=T_{2\beta,t}^*y$ and $I_{n+1}=\beta+I_1$. The intervals $I_2,\ldots,I_n$ are disjoint in the original period, and $\beta+I_1$ lies in the copied period. Each full period has the bound <a href="#eq:energy-suffix" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-suffix">[eq:energy-suffix]</a>, so $$\sum_{j=1}^n\int_{I_{j+1}}\mathcal D_t(Y_j(t))\,dt\le2e_y.$$ Cauchy–Schwarz across the block indices in <a href="#eq:blockaverage" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:blockaverage">[eq:blockaverage]</a>, followed by <a href="#eq:lastblockcorrection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lastblockcorrection">[eq:lastblockcorrection]</a>, gives $$\label{eq:smoothdualcoefficient}
 \left|\sum_{j=1}^n\langle m_j,y\rangle\right|
 \le C\left(\sum_{j=1}^nF_j/L+K_n\right)^{1/2}\sqrt{e_y}
       +C_0\rho(\beta).$$ The sum of localization errors is still rapidly small because $n\le C\beta/L$.

<a id="eq:smoothdualexpectation"></a>
<a id="eq:copycorrectionexpectation"></a>
The expectation of the first term in parentheses is bounded by $C\beta/L^2$ times the right side of <a href="#eq:blockflowexpectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:blockflowexpectation">[eq:blockflowexpectation]</a>. Substituting $\lambda=\beta^{-3/2}$, $D_*=\sqrt\beta$, and $b_*=\beta^{-1}R^{-3}$, this is $$\label{eq:smoothdualexpectation}
 C\left[
 L^4\beta^{-3}
 +L^{-1/2}\beta^{-4}R
 +L^3\beta^{-11/2}R
 +\frac1{\beta R}\right].$$ Likewise, <a href="#eq:lastblocknorm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lastblocknorm">[eq:lastblocknorm]</a> and the one-point snapshot bound give $$\label{eq:copycorrectionexpectation}
 \mathbb E_P[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G}K_n]
 \le C\left(L^5\beta^{-4}+L^5\beta^{-2}R^{-3}\right).$$ Thus the block argument has converted cancellation of source mass into a dual energy bound. It remains to handle the early point-profile sources, for which cancellation at the scale $L$ was not used.

<a id="pm-h-33"></a>
#### Early point-profile sources

<a id="stmt-lem-earlysourceenergy"></a>
<div id="lem:earlysourceenergy" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:earlymassmoment"></a>
<a id="eq:earlydualbound"></a>
**Lemma 23** (Diffusion of the early sources). *Let $\mu_d^{\mathrm{early}}$ be the terminal mass of the point-profile obstacle sources at elapsed times in $[0,\beta/2)$, and let $M$ be their total variation before propagation. Then $$\label{eq:earlymassmoment}
 \mathbb E_P[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G}M^2]\le H_\beta\beta^{-3/2}.$$ For every test vector of Proposition <a href="#prop:dualenergy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dualenergy">21</a>, on $\mathcal G$, $$\label{eq:earlydualbound}
 \lvert \langle \mu_d^{\mathrm{early}},y\rangle\rvert
 \le CM\beta^{-3/4}\sqrt{e_y}+C_0\rho(\beta).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-earlysourceenergy"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-earlysourceenergy</summary>

*Proof.* For a bin beginning at elapsed time $s$, put $d_s=\sqrt{1+s}$. Lemma <a href="#lem:sourcebins" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sourcebins">17</a> bounds its source variation on $\mathcal G$ by $H_\beta\sum_\alpha W_{d_s}(z_\alpha)$, where the sum is over the obstacle slots above $B_{4R}(x)$ at that bin’s starting slice. The single-box case of Lemma <a href="#lem:boxmoment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:boxmoment">20</a> gives $$\begin{aligned}
 \left\|\sum_\alpha W_{d_s}(z_\alpha)\right\|_{L^2(\mathbb P_P)}
 &\le C\left(\sqrt\lambda\,\lVert W_{d_s}\rVert_2
                +\lambda\lVert W_{d_s}\rVert_1\right)\\
 &\le C\left(\beta^{-3/4}d_s^{-5/2}
                 +\beta^{-3/2}d_s^{-1}\right)=J(d_s).
\end{aligned}$$ Minkowski’s inequality in the probability space now gives $$\|\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G}M\|_{L^2(\mathbb P_P)}
 \le H_\beta\sum_{\mathrm{bins}}J(d_s)
 \le H_\beta\beta^{-3/4}.$$ For the last inequality, bins have lengths between $1$ and $3$, so the sum of $d_s^{-5/2}=(1+s)^{-5/4}$ is bounded and the sum of $d_s^{-1}=(1+s)^{-1/2}$ is at most $C\sqrt\beta$. Absorbing the square of a logarithmic factor into $H_\beta$ proves <a href="#eq:earlymassmoment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:earlymassmoment">[eq:earlymassmoment]</a>. This use of Minkowski requires no independence of the bin snapshots.

Propagate all these sources to a slice in a block contained in the last quarter of the traversal. Every propagation has elapsed time at least $\beta/4$. Localize to $B_{6R}(x)$; by Lemma <a href="#lem:localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:localization">12</a>, the discarded total variation is rapidly small on $\mathcal G$. Lemma <a href="#lem:heatbound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:heatbound">11</a> and substochasticity show that a localized unit point mass has total mass at most one and maximum entry at most $C\beta^{-3/2}$. The additional spatial restriction only decreases the kernel. Consequently the localized signed source mass $\nu_t$ is dominated in absolute value by a nonnegative mass $\sigma_t$ such that $$\lVert \sigma_t\rVert_1\le M,
 \qquad
 \lVert \sigma_t\rVert_\infty\le CM\beta^{-3/2}.$$

At a fully regular such slice, use the unit flows of Lemma <a href="#lem:flowoverlap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:flowoverlap">13</a>. For every slot $a$, splitting distances at $D_*$ gives $$\begin{aligned}
 \sum_{a'}\frac{\sigma_t(a')}{1+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(a,a')}
 &\le CM\beta^{-3/2}D_*^2+CM D_*^{-1}\\
 &\le CM\beta^{-1/2}.
\end{aligned}$$ Superposing the unit flows with coefficients $\nu_t(a)$ therefore gives a flow with divergence $\nu_t$ and squared norm at most $$C\sum_{a,a'}\frac{\sigma_t(a)\sigma_t(a')}
                         {1+\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(a,a')}
 \le CM^2\beta^{-1/2}.$$ There are blocks of total length at least $c\beta$ contained in the last quarter, and at least half of each is fully regular. Average <a href="#eq:flowpairing-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flowpairing-energy">[eq:flowpairing-energy]</a> over this regular set. Cauchy–Schwarz in time and <a href="#eq:energy-suffix" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy-suffix">[eq:energy-suffix]</a> give <a href="#eq:earlydualbound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:earlydualbound">[eq:earlydualbound]</a>. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-earlysourceenergy-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-earlysourceenergy</summary>

*Proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:dualenergy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dualenergy">21</a>.* The two collections exhaust the sources in the chosen direction. Combine <a href="#eq:smoothdualcoefficient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:smoothdualcoefficient">[eq:smoothdualcoefficient]</a> and <a href="#eq:earlydualbound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:earlydualbound">[eq:earlydualbound]</a>, and choose a measurable coefficient with $$X_d^2\le C\left(\sum_{j=1}^nF_j/L+K_n+M^2\beta^{-3/2}\right).$$ All expressions on the right are measurable functions of the exposure; none depends on the test vector. Equations <a href="#eq:smoothdualexpectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:smoothdualexpectation">[eq:smoothdualexpectation]</a>, <a href="#eq:copycorrectionexpectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:copycorrectionexpectation">[eq:copycorrectionexpectation]</a>, and <a href="#eq:earlymassmoment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:earlymassmoment">[eq:earlymassmoment]</a> bound its expectation on $\mathcal G$. With $L=\beta^\delta$ and $R=\beta^{3/2+\delta/4}$, their sum is at most <a id="eq:energyfinalpowers"></a>$$\label{eq:energyfinalpowers}
 C\left[
 \beta^{-3+4\delta}
 +\beta^{-5/2-\delta/4}
 +\beta^{-4+13\delta/4}
 +\beta^{-4+5\delta}
 +\beta^{-13/2+17\delta/4}
 +H_\beta\beta^{-3}\right]
 =o(\beta^{-5/2}),$$ since $\delta=1/200$. The term $\beta^{-5/2-\delta/4}$ covers both the isolated net-mass flows and the cutoff flows. All localization errors were estimated in $\ell^1$ against test vectors of supremum norm at most a constant times $C_0$, so they give the uniform rapidly small remainder in <a href="#eq:dualenergy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dualenergy">[eq:dualenergy]</a>. The argument used elapsed time and the ordered kernels throughout; it applies to $U$ and to $U^*$ with the same bounds. Adding the two expected squares proves <a href="#eq:dualenergyexpectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dualenergyexpectation">[eq:dualenergyexpectation]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:pressure"></a>
### From the pinned comparison to magnetization

We first combine the two residual estimates to prove the uniform pinned comparison. We then express a positive field as a mixture of deterministic pin sets. Integrating the finite-volume magnetization bounds in the field will preserve the required order: volume tends to infinity before the field tends to zero.

<a id="pm-h-35"></a>
#### Completion of the capacity comparison

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-earlysourceenergy-3"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-earlysourceenergy</summary>

<a id="eq:returncomparison"></a>
*Proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:comparison">5</a>.* Fix the cube, pins, and starting slot in the proposition, and expose the cycles under $\mathbb P_P$, $P=E\cup\{i\}$. Use the trial vector $h$ and constant $c$ from <a href="#eq:trialvector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:trialvector">[eq:trialvector]</a> and <a href="#eq:freepotential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:freepotential">[eq:freepotential]</a>. On the good event $\mathcal G$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:good" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:good">10</a>, Proposition <a href="#prop:dualenergy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dualenergy">21</a>, applied with a fixed supremum bound $2$, gives a measurable $X$ such that $$\lvert \langle \mu_d,z\rangle\rvert\le X\sqrt{e_z}+\varepsilon_\beta,
 \qquad d=+,-,$$ for vectors vanishing on killed cut slots with $\lVert z\rVert_\infty\le2$, where $\varepsilon_\beta$ is rapidly small and $$\mathbb E_P[\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G}X^2]=o(\beta^{-5/2}).$$ If the two directions initially give different coefficients, take their maximum; this preserves the displayed expectation bound. The source identities <a href="#eq:residualdefinition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:residualdefinition">[eq:residualdefinition]</a> identify these residuals with those in <a href="#eq:capacityresiduals" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:capacityresiduals">[eq:capacityresiduals]</a>. Since $0\le h\le1$, the averaged capacity criterion <a href="#eq:averagedcapacity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:averagedcapacity">[eq:averagedcapacity]</a> applies. Proposition <a href="#prop:scalar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:scalar">19</a> and the probability estimate in Proposition <a href="#prop:good" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:good">10</a> give $$\label{eq:returncomparison}
 r:=\mathbb E_Pq_i=1-c+o(\beta^{-5/2}).$$ Every error here is uniform over the cube and the allowed pin sets. In particular, the good-event estimate is used only through its probability on the complement; there $q_i\in[0,1]$ suffices.

<a id="eq:slotcomparison"></a>
By Lemma <a href="#lem:freebounds" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:freebounds">14</a>, $v(x)=O(\beta^{-3/2})$, so $$1-c=\frac{v(x)}{1+v(x)}=v(x)+O(\beta^{-3}).$$ The exact pin identity <a href="#eq:pinreturn" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pinreturn">[eq:pinreturn]</a> now yields $$\label{eq:slotcomparison}
 d_E(i)=\frac{r}{1+r}
       =v(x)+o(\beta^{-5/2})
       =\frac1\ell\sum_{n\ge1}p_{n\beta}(0)
          +o(\beta^{-5/2}).$$ Indeed $r=O(\beta^{-3/2})$, so the second rational transformation also costs only $O(\beta^{-3})$. By <a href="#eq:freeidealdensity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:freeidealdensity">[eq:freeidealdensity]</a>, the last sum is $n_S^{\rm sw}(\beta)$. The uniform little-oh in <a href="#eq:slotcomparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slotcomparison">[eq:slotcomparison]</a> is equivalent to the asserted function $\varepsilon_S(\beta)\to0$ after choosing a sufficiently large $\beta_0(S)$. Equations <a href="#eq:returncomparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:returncomparison">[eq:returncomparison]</a> and <a href="#eq:freeidealdensity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:freeidealdensity">[eq:freeidealdensity]</a>, together with $1-c=v(x)+O(\beta^{-3})$, also give $r=\ell^{-1}n_S^{\rm sw}(\beta)+o(\beta^{-5/2})$, as asserted. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-36"></a>
#### Boundary pins and field pins

<a id="eq:pinnedpressure"></a>
Write $V_N=|\Lambda_N|$, use $\partial\Lambda_N=\partial_{\mathrm{in}}\Lambda_N$ for the inner boundary, and let $q_{\Lambda_N}$ be the number of slot edges. For a picture $\omega$, let $\mathcal C(\omega)$ be its cycles and $a_C$ the number of time-zero slots on $C$. For colors $\sigma_C\in\{-1,1\}$, define $$M(\omega,\sigma)=\frac12\sum_Ca_C\sigma_C,
 \qquad
 N_\downarrow(\omega,\sigma)=\sum_{C:\,\sigma_C=-1}a_C
                              =SV_N-M(\omega,\sigma).$$ Let $\chi_D(\omega,\sigma)$ indicate that every cycle meeting $D$ is up. The spin-loop representation in Proposition <a href="#prop:loopinputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:loopinputs">3</a> identifies the ordinary partition function with $$Z_N(t)=e^{\beta q_{\Lambda_N}/4}
       \mathbb E_{\rm b}\sum_\sigma e^{\beta tM(\omega,\sigma)},
 \qquad p_N(t)=\frac{\log Z_N(t)}{\beta V_N}.$$ Its boundary-pinned counterpart and pressure are $$\label{eq:pinnedpressure}
 Z_N^+(t)=e^{\beta q_{\Lambda_N}/4}
       \mathbb E_{\rm b}\sum_\sigma
          \chi_{D_0}(\omega,\sigma)e^{\beta tM(\omega,\sigma)},
 \qquad
 p_N^+(t)=\frac{\log Z_N^+(t)}{\beta V_N}.$$

We use the surface estimate of <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[BlochLaw](#ref-BlochLaw), Proposition 7.1]. Its argument is short and records why all-times boundary pins do not change the pressure.

<a id="stmt-lem-surfacecost"></a>
<div id="lem:surfacecost" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:surfacecost"></a>
**Lemma 24** (Surface cost of boundary pins). *For every finite $\beta>0$, every $t\ge0$, and every finite cube, $$\label{eq:surfacecost}
 0\le p_N(t)-p_N^+(t)
 \le (\log2)\left(\frac\ell\beta+24\ell^2\right)
        \frac{|\partial\Lambda_N|}{V_N}.$$ In particular $p_N^+(t)\to p_{S,\beta}(t)$ for each fixed $\beta>0$ and $t\ge0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-surfacecost"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-surfacecost</summary>

*Proof.* After extracting the all-up factor, the field-tilted picture weight is $$W_t(\omega)=\prod_C(1+e^{-\beta t a_C}).$$ Let $\mathbb E_t$ denote expectation under this tilted picture law. Given the picture, each cycle is up independently with probability $(1+e^{-\beta t a_C})^{-1}\ge1/2$. If $b_0$ counts cycles meeting $D_0$, Jensen’s inequality therefore gives $$1\ge\frac{Z_N^+(t)}{Z_N(t)}
   \ge\mathbb E_t2^{-b_0}
   \ge\exp[-(\log2)\mathbb E_t b_0].$$ Let $J_\partial$ count marks on slot edges incident to a boundary site, and let $q_\partial$ be the number of those edges. Every boundary-meeting cycle either has a boundary slot at time zero or passes through one of these marks. A mark belongs to at most two cycles, so $$b_0\le\ell|\partial\Lambda_N|+2J_\partial,
 \qquad q_\partial\le6\ell^2|\partial\Lambda_N|.$$ Insertion of one mark joins two cycles or splits one. Each factor of $W_t$ lies in $[1,2]$, and at most two new factors appear, whence $W_t(\omega\cup\{u\})\le4W_t(\omega)$ for almost every inserted mark $u$. The Poisson insertion formula at rate $1/2$ implies $$\mathbb E_t J_\partial\le2\beta q_\partial
               \le12\beta\ell^2|\partial\Lambda_N|.$$ The preceding inequalities, divided by $\beta V_N$, prove <a href="#eq:surfacecost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:surfacecost">[eq:surfacecost]</a>. The pressure limit follows because the boundary-to-volume ratio tends to zero. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:fieldpinidentity"></a>
<a id="eq:fieldjointlaw"></a>
For $t\ge0$ put $b_\beta(t)=1-e^{-\beta t}$, and let $\nu_b$ be the law that selects each time-zero slot independently with probability $b=b_\beta(t)$. If $A$ is the selected set and $\operatorname{Up}(\omega,\sigma)$ is the set of up slots at that cut, then $$\label{eq:fieldpinidentity}
 \sum_A\nu_b(A)\mathbf 1_{\{A\subset\operatorname{Up}\}}
   =(1-b)^{N_\downarrow}=e^{-\beta tN_\downarrow}.$$ Consequently the field law in <a href="#eq:pinnedpressure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pinnedpressure">[eq:pinnedpressure]</a> is the picture/color marginal of the joint law $$\label{eq:fieldjointlaw}
 \widehat\mathbb P_{N,t}(d\omega,d\sigma,A)
  \ \propto\ \mathbb P_{\rm b}(d\omega)\,\nu_b(A)
                         \chi_{D_0\cup A}(\omega,\sigma),$$ where the color variable uses counting measure. Indeed the common factor extracted from its normalizer is $e^{\beta q_{\Lambda_N}/4+\beta tSV_N}$. Conditional on $A$, this is exactly the zero-field color law with the deterministic pins $E=D_0\cup A$.

<a id="eq:fieldpinbound"></a>
The marginal law of $A$ is generally not Bernoulli, because its normalization is weighted by the pinned partition function. Conditioning instead on $(\omega,\sigma)$ makes selections on up slots independent Bernoulli choices of parameter $b$, and forbids selections on down slots. Thus every specified slot $j$ satisfies $$\label{eq:fieldpinbound}
 \widehat\mathbb P_{N,t}(j\in A)
   =b_\beta(t)\widehat\mathbb P_{N,t}(j\text{ up})\le b_\beta(t).$$ This is the only property of the posterior pin law needed below.

<a id="pm-h-37"></a>
#### Thermodynamic and zero-field limits

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-surfacecost-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-surfacecost</summary>

<a id="eq:pressuredeficit"></a>
<a id="eq:derivativecomparison"></a>
*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Fix $S$ and a sufficiently large $\beta$. The finite-volume magnetization is bounded, so differentiation under the sum in <a href="#eq:pinnedpressure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pinnedpressure">[eq:pinnedpressure]</a> is justified. Equations <a href="#eq:fieldjointlaw" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fieldjointlaw">[eq:fieldjointlaw]</a> and <a href="#eq:fieldpinidentity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fieldpinidentity">[eq:fieldpinidentity]</a> give $$\label{eq:pressuredeficit}
 S-(p_N^+)'(t)
  =\frac1{V_N}\widehat\mathbb E_{N,t}N_\downarrow
 =\frac1{V_N}\sum_{j\text{ at time }0}
                         \widehat\mathbb E_{N,t}d_{D_0\cup A}(j).$$ Here $d_E(j)$ means the down probability also for pinned slots, where it is zero. Let $\rho_N$ be the fraction of sites $x$ for which $B_{50R_*}(x)$ is interior to $\Lambda_N$. For such a site let $\mathcal A_x$ be the event that no selected pin lies above that ball. A union bound using <a href="#eq:fieldpinbound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fieldpinbound">[eq:fieldpinbound]</a> gives $$\widehat\mathbb P_{N,t}(\mathcal A_x^c)
 \le C_{S,\beta}\,b_\beta(t),$$ where $C_{S,\beta}$ is finite and independent of $N$. On $\mathcal A_x$, Proposition <a href="#prop:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:comparison">5</a> applies to each of the $\ell$ slots at $x$, for every conditional pin set $A$. On its complement, and at sites outside the interior region, use the boundedness of down probabilities. Summing the slot estimates in <a href="#eq:pressuredeficit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pressuredeficit">[eq:pressuredeficit]</a> gives $$\label{eq:derivativecomparison}
 \lvert S-(p_N^+)'(t)-n_S^{\rm sw}(\beta)\rvert
 \le \ell\varepsilon_S(\beta)\beta^{-5/2}
       +C_S(1-\rho_N)+C_{S,\beta}b_\beta(t).$$ Here $n_S^{\rm sw}(\beta)$ is bounded for the large values of $\beta$ under consideration, so its contribution on exceptional sites is included in $C_S$.

Integrate <a href="#eq:derivativecomparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:derivativecomparison">[eq:derivativecomparison]</a> over $t\in[0,h]$, for $h>0$, and divide by $h$. Now take $N\to\infty$, keeping $\beta$ and $h$ fixed. The radius is fixed in this limit, so $\rho_N\to1$, and Lemma <a href="#lem:surfacecost" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:surfacecost">24</a> gives convergence of $p_N^+$ at both field endpoints. We obtain $$\lvert S-\frac{p_{S,\beta}(h)-p_{S,\beta}(0)}h
                    -n_S^{\rm sw}(\beta)\rvert
 \le \ell\varepsilon_S(\beta)\beta^{-5/2}
       +\frac{C_{S,\beta}}h\int_0^h b_\beta(t)\,dt.$$ For this fixed $\beta$, the last term tends to zero as $h\downarrow0$. Convexity and the uniform magnetization bound give the right derivative of the limiting pressure. Therefore $$\lvert S-m_S(\beta)-n_S^{\rm sw}(\beta)\rvert
 \le\ell\varepsilon_S(\beta)\beta^{-5/2}.$$ Finally let $\beta\to\infty$. Since $S$ is fixed, multiplication by $(\beta S)^{5/2}$ proves the theorem. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-38"></a>
#### The explicit lattice correction

The full ideal density has a two-term expansion with a remainder small enough to identify the coefficient in Corollary <a href="#cor:expansion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:expansion">2</a>.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-surfacecost-3"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-surfacecost</summary>

<a id="eq:laplaceexpansion"></a>
*Proof of Corollary <a href="#cor:expansion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:expansion">2</a>.* Put $t=\beta S$. Independence of the three coordinates gives $$p_\beta(0)=I(t)^3,\qquad
 I(t)=\frac1{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}
                         e^{-2t(1-\cos k)}\,dk.$$ We claim, uniformly for $t\ge1$, that $$\label{eq:laplaceexpansion}
 I(t)=\frac1{\sqrt{4\pi t}}
          \left(1+\frac1{16t}+O(t^{-2})\right).$$ To see this, set $u=\sqrt t\,k$ and first restrict to $|u|\le t^{1/10}$. Taylor’s formula gives $$2t(1-\cos(u/\sqrt t))
       =u^2-\frac{u^4}{12t}+O(u^6/t^2),$$ and hence, on this interval, $$e^{-2t(1-\cos(u/\sqrt t))}
 =e^{-u^2}\left(1+\frac{u^4}{12t}
                   +O\!\left(\frac{u^6+u^8}{t^2}\right)\right).$$ The bound $2(1-\cos k)\ge c k^2$ controls the omitted interval by $O(e^{-c t^{1/5}})$ after the same rescaling. Extending the Gaussian integrals to $\mathbb R$ has an error of this size as well. Since $$\int_\mathbb Re^{-u^2}\,du=\sqrt\pi,
 \qquad
 \int_\mathbb Ru^4e^{-u^2}\,du=\frac34\sqrt\pi,$$ the formula <a href="#eq:laplaceexpansion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:laplaceexpansion">[eq:laplaceexpansion]</a> follows, with a constant that can be enlarged to cover all $t\ge1$. Cubing gives $$p_\beta(0)
   =(4\pi t)^{-3/2}\left(1+\frac3{16t}+O(t^{-2})\right).$$ Apply this uniform estimate at $nt$, $n\ge1$, and sum. The remainder is bounded by $Ct^{-7/2}\sum_{n\ge1}n^{-7/2}$, so <a id="eq:idealdensityexpansion"></a>$$\label{eq:idealdensityexpansion}
 n_S^{\rm sw}(\beta)
  =\frac{\zeta(3/2)}{8\pi^{3/2}}(\beta S)^{-3/2}
   +\frac{3\zeta(5/2)}{128\pi^{3/2}}(\beta S)^{-5/2}
   +O((\beta S)^{-7/2}).$$ Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> now gives the asserted expansion of $S-m_S(\beta)$. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The color-counting comparison for a fixed isolated strand. Time runs upwards; horizontal segments represent instantaneous exchanges, and the slot coordinates are schematic. Recoloring the prescribed segment $X$ up allows its jump marks to be removed and its incident holding intervals to be filled freely. The only remaining color discontinuities are the two directed endpoint flips. Each cycle meeting a flip has at most one admissible coloring, which gives the pin-weight bound used in the path comparison.",
      "figure_id": "fig:surgery",
      "pdf_page": 12,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:surgery",
          "exact_quote": "The color-counting comparison for a fixed isolated strand.\nTime runs upwards; horizontal segments represent instantaneous exchanges,\nand the slot coordinates are schematic. Recoloring the prescribed segment\n$X$ up allows its jump marks to b",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.86cm,y=0.88cm,>=Stealth,\n every node/.style={font=\\small}]\n \\definecolor{downpath}{RGB}{165,55,50}\n \\definecolor{upfill}{RGB}{25,108,105}\n \\begin{scope}\n  \\node[font=\\small\\bfseries] at (1.5,4.5) {Fixed isolated down strand};\n  \\foreach \\x in {0,1,2,3}\n   \\draw[gray!40] (\\x,0.25)--(\\x,3.8);\n  \\draw[->,gray] (-0.65,0.35)--(-0.65,3.7) node[above] {$t$};\n  \\draw[downpath,very thick] (1,0.35)--(1,1.15)--(2,1.15)--(2,2.55)--(1,2.55)--(1,3.7);\n  \\draw[downpath,->,thick] (1.2,1.15)--(1.85,1.15);\n  \\draw[downpath,->,thick] (1.8,2.55)--(1.15,2.55);\n  \\fill[downpath] (1,0.35) circle (2pt);\n  \\fill[downpath] (1,3.7) circle (2pt);\n  \\node[left] at (0,0.35) {$t_0$};\n  \\node[left] at (0,3.7) {$t_1$};\n  \\node[right,align=left,text=downpath] at (2.2,1.6) {$X$};\n  \\node[align=center] at (1.5,-0.35) {The incident holding intervals\\\\contain no exchange marks.};\n \\end{scope}\n \\begin{scope}[xshift=6.8cm]\n  \\node[font=\\small\\bfseries] at (1.5,4.5) {Recolor, delete, and fill};\n  \\foreach \\x in {0,1,2,3}\n   \\draw[upfill!45] (\\x,0.25)--(\\x,3.8);\n  \\draw[gray,dashed,thick] (1,0.35)--(1,1.15)--(2,1.15)--(2,2.55)--(1,2.55)--(1,3.7);\n  \\foreach \\x/\\y in {0/0.8,1/1.7,2/2.1,0/3.15}\n   \\draw[upfill,very thick] (\\x,\\y)--++(1,0);\n  \\draw[upfill,fill=white,thick] (1,0.35) circle (3pt);\n  \\draw[upfill,fill=white,thick] (1,3.7) circle (3pt);\n  \\node[right,fill=white,inner sep=2pt] at (1.1,0.35) {down $\\to$ up};\n  \\node[right,fill=white,inner sep=2pt] at (1.1,3.7) {up $\\to$ down};\n  \\node[align=center] at (1.5,-0.35) {New incident marks exchange\\\\two up colors.};\n \\end{scope}\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Two-panel schematic diagram. Left panel titled \"Fixed isolated down strand\": vertical time axis $t$ directed upwards with ticks $t_0$ and $t_1$; three vertical lines representing slot coordinates; a highlighted strand in red traversing between slots via horizontal segments (instantaneous exchanges), with a vertical segment between $t_0$ and $t_1$ labeled $X$; text below: \"The incident holding intervals contain no exchange marks.\" Right panel titled \"Recolor, delete, and fill\": three vertical slot lines with solid and dashed segments; top endpoint flip with curved arrow labeled \"up $\\to$ down\"; bottom endpoint flip with curved arrow labeled \"down $\\to$ up\"; text below: \"New incident marks exchange two up colors.\""
    },
    {
      "caption": "Averaging the source mass from one block in the next block. The last block uses the first block of a copied period. The displayed identity separates the pairing of its terminal mass $m$ into a term controlled by contraction energy and a term whose propagation can be split at the copied averaging slices. The averaging windows are disjoint modulo one period, so their Dirichlet energies are summed with a constant total cost. Block widths are schematic.",
      "figure_id": "fig:period-averaging",
      "pdf_page": 31,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:period-averaging",
          "exact_quote": "Averaging the source mass from one block in the next block.\nThe last block uses the first block of a copied period. The displayed identity\nseparates the pairing of its terminal mass $m$ into a term controlled by contraction energy\nand a ter",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.9cm,y=0.9cm,>=Stealth,\n every node/.style={font=\\small}]\n \\definecolor{flowblue}{RGB}{25,108,105}\n \\foreach \\a/\\b/\\lab in {0/1.3/I_1,2.5/3.8/I_j,3.8/5.1/I_{j+1},6.4/7.7/I_n}\n  {\\draw[fill=gray!7] (\\a,0) rectangle (\\b,0.75);\n   \\node at ({(\\a+\\b)/2},0.375) {$\\lab$};}\n \\node at (1.9,0.375) {$\\cdots$};\n \\node at (5.75,0.375) {$\\cdots$};\n \\draw[fill=flowblue!10] (7.7,0) rectangle (9.5,0.75);\n \\node at (8.6,0.375) {$I_1+\\beta$};\n \\node at (10.05,0.375) {$\\cdots$};\n \\draw[->] (-0.25,-0.25)--(10.65,-0.25) node[right] {$t$};\n \\draw (0,-0.2)--(0,-0.3) node[below] {$0$};\n \\draw (7.7,-0.2)--(7.7,-0.3) node[below] {$\\beta$};\n \\draw[dashed] (7.7,-0.05)--(7.7,2.1);\n \\draw[->,thick,flowblue] (3.15,0.9) to[out=70,in=110] (4.45,0.9);\n \\node[align=center,text=flowblue] at (3.8,1.9) {propagate, then average\\\\on regular slices};\n \\draw[->,thick,flowblue] (7.05,0.9) to[out=70,in=110] (8.6,0.9);\n \\node[align=center,text=flowblue] at (8.5,2.55) {continue through\\\\the next period};\n \\node[align=center] at (5.1,-1.3)\n  {$\\langle m,y\\rangle=\\langle m,y-U_d^*y\\rangle+\\langle U_dm,y\\rangle$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Timeline diagram along a horizontal time axis $t$ pointing right, showing coordinates $0$ and $\\beta$. Contiguous rectangular blocks labeled $I_1$, $\\dots$, $I_j$, $I_{j+1}$, $\\dots$, $I_n$, $I_1 + \\beta$, $\\dots$. A green curved arrow arches from block $I_j$ to block $I_{j+1}$ labeled \"propagate, then average on regular slices\". A vertical dashed line with an upward arrow at $t = \\beta$ (boundary between $I_n$ and $I_1 + \\beta$) labeled \"continue through the next period\". Below the blocks, a centered identity is displayed: $\\langle m, y \\rangle = \\langle m, y - U_d^* y \\rangle + \\langle U_d m, y \\rangle$."
    }
